(H1a), (H5) and (H6) imply the existence of a specification

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)})
such that

\textbf{(H9)}\quad\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}

•

Instantiating (H9) with (H1), (H7) and (H3) yields

\textbf{(H11)}\quad\begin{array}[]{l}\forall s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\ldotp(s^{\prime\prime},h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}ok:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\\
\quad\Rightarrow(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\wedge\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime})\end{array}

•

Defining
v^{\prime}:=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}
, we apply the operation semantics of the successfully termination function call, which yields

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime\prime})

•

To conclude the proof, it remains to show that
\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime}\models\vec{\mathsf{E}}[\mathit{E}_{y}/\mathsf{y}]=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}Q_{\mathit{ok}}[\mathsf{y}/\mathsf{ret}]
.
(H11) implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}
. Defining
\vec{\mathsf{p}}:=\mathtt{pv}(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime})
and
\vec{v}:=s^{\prime}(\vec{\mathsf{p}})
, we obtain
\theta[\vec{p}\rightarrow\vec{v}],-,h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
where
-
may denote any variable store, since the assertion does not hold any program variables. Therefore,
\theta[\vec{p}\rightarrow\vec{v}],s,h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
and hence
(H12)

\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime}\models\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{y}=v^{\prime}
hold.

•

From the definitions of
v^{\prime}
,
\vec{\mathsf{p}}
and
\vec{p}
we obtain
v^{\prime}:=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]}\rrbracket_{\theta,-}
and therefore
\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime}\models\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{E}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
. Hence
\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}[\mathsf{y}/\mathsf{ret}]
.

•

From (H2a) and Lemma
3
, we obtain
\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]\models\vec{\mathsf{E}}[\mathit{E}_{y}/\mathsf{y}]=\vec{x}
and therefore
\theta,s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}],h^{\prime}\models\vec{\mathsf{E}}[\mathit{E}_{y}/\mathsf{y}]=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}Q_{\mathit{ok}}[\mathsf{y}/\mathsf{ret}]
, which concludes this case of the proof.

Finally, we prove the erroneously terminating over-approximation soundness. Our hypotheses are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P

(H3)

h~\sharp~h_{f}

(H4)

\mathsf{y}\not\in\mathtt{fv}(\mathit{E}_{y})

(H5)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathcal{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mathit{dm}(\Gamma(f))
.

Our goal is to show that:

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\ldotp(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}err:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\\
\quad\quad\Rightarrow(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}Q_{\mathit{err}})\end{array}

•

From (H1) and Lemma
20
, we obtain
(H1a)

\models(\gamma,\mathcal{A}\mkern-1.0mu\mathit{dm}(\Gamma))

•

From (H1a) and (H5), we obtain
C
and
\mathsf{E}
such that
(H6)

f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{C,\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\}\in\gamma
.

•

We define
\vec{v}:=\theta(\vec{x})
and obtain from (H2), that
(H2a)

\theta,s\models\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}
and
\theta,s,h\models P
, and hence (H2b)
\theta,s[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}],h\models\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P
.

•

Since
\mathtt{pv}(P)=\emptyset
we obtain from (H2b) with Lemma
1
that
\theta,\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}],h\models\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P
and
(H7)

\theta,\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}],h\models\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
.

•

(H1a), (H5) and (H6) imply the existence of a specification

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)})
such that

\textbf{(H9)}\quad\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}

•

Instantiating (H9) with (H1), (H7) and (H3) yields

\textbf{(H11)}\quad\begin{array}[]{l}\forall s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\ldotp(s^{\prime\prime},h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}err:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\\
\quad\Rightarrow(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\wedge\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime})\end{array}

•

Defining
v_{err}:=\llbracket{\mathsf{err}}\rrbracket_{s^{\prime}}
, we apply the operation semantics of the erroneously termination function call, which yields

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime\prime})

•

To conclude the proof, it remains to show that
\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}Q_{\mathit{err}}
.

\theta,s^{\prime}\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}
holds trivially and (H11) implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}
. Defining
\vec{\mathsf{p}}:=\mathtt{pv}(Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime})
and
\vec{v}:=s^{\prime}(\vec{\mathsf{p}})
, we obtain
\theta[\vec{p}\rightarrow\vec{v}],-,h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
where
-
may denote any variable store, since the assertion does not hold any program variables. Therefore,
\theta[\vec{p}\rightarrow\vec{v}],s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
and hence
\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime}\models\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
and
(H12)

\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}
holds.

•

(H2) implies
\theta(\vec{x})=\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}=\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}]}
and hence
\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\mapsto v_{err}]\models\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}
. Therefore, we obtain
\theta,s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}Q_{\mathit{err}}
, which concludes the proof.

While.
The while rule is:

\frac{\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}\quad\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array})}}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{0}\end{array})}\ {\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ \mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\neg\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}\exists i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp P_{i}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp Q_{i}\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{while}}

We prove the under-approximation case for successful termination; the faulting case is proven analogously. Our hypotheses are as follows:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
;

(H2a)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}

(H2b)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array}\right)}
;

(H3)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\neg\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}\exists i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp P_{i}
;

(H4)

{h^{\prime}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{f}}
.

Our goal is to show that:

\exists s,h\ldotp\theta,s,h\models P_{0}\land(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}ok:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

•

(H3) implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\neg\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}P_{i}
and therefore there exists some
t\in\mathbb{N}
such that
(H5)

\theta[i\mapsto t],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\neg\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}P_{i}
, which implies
(H6)

\theta[i\mapsto t],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models P_{i}
.

•

If
t=0
, (H5) implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models P_{0}
and
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s^{\prime}}=\mathtt{false}
. The operational semantics then yields
(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ \mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})
which implies the desired result.

•

Otherwise, we have
t>0
, and the iterated application of (H2a), (H2b), and the induction hypothesis yields the existence of some
s
and
h
such that
(H7)

\theta[i\mapsto t],s,h\models P_{0}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}
and
(H8)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}^{t}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})
. (H7) also implies
\theta,s,h\models P_{0}
. Finally, given (H2a), (H8), and the operational semantics of the while loop, we also obtain the desired
(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ \mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})
.

Second, we prove the over-approximating case. Our hypotheses are as follows:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
;

(H2a)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}

(H2b)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array}\right)}
;

(H3)

\theta,s,h\models P_{0}
,

(H4)

{h\mathbin{\sharp}h_{f}}
.

and our goal is to show that:

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\ldotp(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\implies\\
\qquad\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land((o=\mathit{ok}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\neg\mathsf{E}\land\exists i\ldotp P_{i})\lor(o=\mathit{err}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists i.~Q_{i}))\end{array}

Taking
(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
, we have the following:

•

If
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{false}
, the operational semantics yield that
o=\mathit{ok}
,
s^{\prime}=s
and
h^{\prime\prime}=h_{f}\uplus h
, which, together with (H3) implies the desired result.

•

If
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}
, (H5) implies that there exists a
t>0
such that
(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}^{t}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
and (H2a), (H2b), and the inductive hypothesis imply
(H6)

\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land((o=\mathit{ok}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models P_{t})\lor(o=\mathit{err}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\bigvee_{i=0}^{t-1}Q_{i}))
and since the final states
(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
coincide for (H5) and (H6) given the operational semantics of the while loop, we have the desired goal.

Frame.
The frame rule is:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}\qquad\mathtt{mod}(C)\cap\mathtt{fv}(R)=\emptyset}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{frame}}

To prove the soundness of this rule, our hypotheses are that for arbitrary
\gamma
:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}

(H3)

\mathtt{mod}(C)\cap\mathtt{fv}(R)=\emptyset

From the inductive hypothesis and
(H2)
, it follows that
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}

(H4)
.
It then suffices to show that
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\mathrel{\star}R\end{array}\right)}
holds.
We start off by showing the over-approximating case. For this we assume that for some
\theta,s,h,h_{f},o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
:

(H5)

\theta,s,h\models P\mathrel{\star}R

(H6)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

From the definition of the satisfiability relation and
(H5)
, it follows that there exists some heaps,
\bar{h}
and
\bar{h}_{r}
, such that:

(H7)

h=\bar{h}\uplus\bar{h}_{r}

(H8)

\theta,s,\bar{h}\models P

(H9)

\theta,s,\bar{h}_{r}\models R

Letting
\bar{h}_{f}=\bar{h}_{r}\uplus h_{f}
, from
(H6)
and the associativity of
\uplus
, it follows that

(s,\bar{h}\uplus\bar{h}_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(H10)
.
From
(H4)
,
(H1)
,
(H8)
and
(H10)
, it follows that:

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus\bar{h}_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}

By applying
\cref
LemMods and
\cref
LemPv to
(H9)
, we can infer that
\theta,s^{\prime},\bar{h}_{r}\models R
.
Then letting
\bar{h}^{\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus\bar{h}_{r}
, given the definition of the satisfiability relation,
it follows that
\theta,s^{\prime},\bar{h}^{\prime}\models Q_{o}\mathrel{\star}R
. We can then infer that:

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus\bar{h}_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}\mathrel{\star}R

as required. We now show the under-approximating case. For this we assume that for some
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},h_{f},o
:

(H11)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}\mathrel{\star}R

(H12)

h_{f}~\sharp~h^{\prime}

From the definition of the satisfiability relation and
(H11)
, it follows that there exists some heaps,
\bar{h}^{\prime}
and
\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}
such that:

(H13)

h^{\prime}=\bar{h}^{\prime}\uplus\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}

(H14)

\theta,s^{\prime},\bar{h}^{\prime}\models Q_{o}

(H15)

\theta,s^{\prime},\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}\models R

Letting
\bar{h}_{f}=h_{f}\uplus\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}
, from
(H12)
and
(H13)
, it follows that
\bar{h}_{f}~\sharp~\bar{h}^{\prime}

(H16)
. From
(H4)
, applying
(H1)
,
(H14)
and
(H16)
, it follows that:

\exists s,\bar{h}\ldotp~\theta,s,\bar{h}\models P~\land~(s,\bar{h}\uplus\bar{h}_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},\bar{h}^{\prime}\uplus\bar{h}_{f})

By applying
\cref
LemMods and
\cref
LemPv to
(H15)
, it follows that
\theta,s,\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}\models R
.
Letting
h=\bar{h}\uplus\bar{h}_{r}^{\prime}
, by the definition of the satisfiability relation, it follows that:

\exists s,\bar{h}\ldotp~\theta,s,h\models P\mathrel{\star}R~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

as required.

Equivalence.
The equivalence rule is

\frac{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\quad\models P^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow P,Q_{\mathit{ok}},Q_{\mathit{err}}}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{equiv}}

Our hypotheses for OX-soundness are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}

(H3)

\models P^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow P,Q_{\mathit{ok}},Q_{\mathit{err}}

(H4)

\theta,s,h\models P

(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

and we aim to show that

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}

(H3) and (H4) implies
\theta,s,h\models P^{\prime}
and with (H2) and (H5) then implies
(H6)

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{\prime}_{o}
. (H2) then implies the desired result.

For the UX-soundness, the hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}

(H3)

\models P^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime},Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow P,Q_{\mathit{ok}},Q_{\mathit{err}}

(H4)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}

(H5)

h^{\prime}~\sharp~h_{f}

(H2) and (H4) implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{\prime}_{o}
. (H2) and (H5) implies

\exists s,h\ldotp\theta,s,h\models P^{\prime}\land(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})
. (H3) then implies the desired result.

Existentials.
The existential rule is:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x.\,P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{exists}}

To prove the soundness of this rule, our hypotheses are that for arbitrary
\gamma
:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}

Using the inductive hypothesis and
(H2)
, it follows that
(H3)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}
.
It then suffices to show that
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x.\,P\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}
.
We start off by showing the over-approximating case. To do so, we assume that for some
\theta,s,h,h_{f},o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
:

(H4)

\theta,s,h\models\exists x.\,P

(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

From
(H4)
and the definition of the satisfiability relation,
it follows that, for some
v
,

(H6)

\theta[x\mapsto v],s,h\models P
holds.
From
(H3)
,
(H1)
,
(H6)
and
(H5)
, it follows that:

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta[x\mapsto v],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}

This trivially entails:

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists x.\,Q_{o}

as required. We now show the under-approximating case.
To do so, we assume that for some
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},h_{f},o
:

(H7)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists x\,Q_{o}

(H8)

h_{f}~\sharp~h^{\prime}

From
(H7)
and the definition of the satisfiability relation, it follows that,
for some
v
,

(H9)

\theta[x\mapsto v],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}
.
From
(H3)
,
(H1)
,
(H9)
and
(H8)
,
it follows that:

\exists s,h\ldotp~\theta[x\mapsto v],s,h\models P~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

and consequently:

\exists s,h\ldotp~\theta,s,h\models\exists x.\,P~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

as required.

Disjunction.
The disjunction rule is

\frac{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{1}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{1}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{1}\end{array})}\quad\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{2}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{2}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{2}\end{array})}}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{1}\lor P_{2}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{1}\lor Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{2}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{1}\lor Q_{\mathit{err}}^{2}\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{disj}}

Our hypotheses for OX-soundness are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{1}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{1}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{1}\end{array})}

(H3)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{2}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{2}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{2}\end{array})}

(H4)

\theta,s,h\models P_{1}\lor P_{2}

(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(H4) implies that
(\theta,s,h\models P_{1})\lor(\theta,s,h\models P_{2})
. If the first case of the disjunct holds, (H2) implies
(H6a)

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}^{1}
. Otherwise, the second case holds and (H3) yields
(H6b)

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}^{2}
.
The disjunction of (H6a) and (H6b) yields the desired result.

For the UX-soundness, our hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{1}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{1}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{1}\end{array})}

(H3)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{2}\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{2}\end{array})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{2}\end{array})}

(H4)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{1}_{o}\lor Q^{2}_{o}

(H5)

h^{\prime}~\sharp~h_{f}

(H4) implies
(\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{1}_{o})\lor(\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{2}_{o})
. If the first case of the disjunct holds, (H2) yields
(H6a)

\exists{s,h}(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})\land\theta,s,h\models P_{1}
. Otherwise, the second case of the disjunction holds and (H3) implies
(H6b)

\exists{s,h}(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})\land\theta,s,h\models P_{2}
. The disjunction of (H6a) and (H6b) yields the desired result.

Lookup.
The Lookup rule is

\frac{\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})\qquad\theta\triangleq[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}_{1}\theta\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\theta\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}\theta\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{lookup}}

To prove its OX-soundness, the hypotheses are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})

(H4)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}

(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

and we aim to show

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}_{1}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]

(H5) yields
(H6)

o\neq\mathit{miss}
,
(H7)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=v
and
(H8)

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{x}\rightarrow v]
and
(H9)

h^{\prime\prime}=h\uplus h_{f}
.
Chosing
(10)
h^{\prime}=h
, (H4) and (H8) yield
(H11)

\theta,s^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}
.
(H4), (H9), (H10) and (H11) imply the desired result.

For UX-soundness, our hypotheses are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})

(H3)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}_{1}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]

(H4)

h^{\prime}~\sharp~h_{f}

and we aim to show:

\exists s,h.\,(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})\land\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}

Letting
v=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}
,
s=s^{\prime}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto v]
and
h=h^{\prime}
, then:

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

holds. Then given (H2), by applying
\cref
LemSubst, it is clear that
v=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}
,
and therefore
\theta,s\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. Finally, similarly,

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}_{1}
, from which we can reach our goal by the definition
of the satisfiability relation.

Lookup-err-val.
The Lookup-err-val rule is

\frac{\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq[``\mathsf{ExprEval}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E})]}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{lookup-err-val}}

where
Q_{\mathit{err}}=\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{err}=\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}
.
To prove OX-soundness, the hypothese are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}

(H3)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

It then suffices to show that
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{err}=\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}
.
Given (H2) and the definition satisfiability relation, it follows that
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s,h}\notin\mathsf{Val}
, and therefore
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s,h}=\lightning
. From this we can infer that the only rule from the big-step operational semantics that
can apply is:

({s},{h}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}{\mathit{err}}:({s_{\mathit{err}}},{h})\begin{array}[]{c}\llbracket{{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\lightning\\
v_{\mathit{err}}=[``\mathsf{ExprEval}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E})]\end{array}

From this, we can infer that
h^{\prime\prime}=h
and
s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{err}\mapsto v_{\mathit{err}}]
.
From (H2) and the definition of the satisfiability relation, it then follows that

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}
as required.

For UX-soundness, our hypotheses are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}

(H3)

h_{f}~\sharp~h^{\prime}

It then suffices to show that for some
s,h
:

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})))

Letting
s=s^{\prime}\setminus\{\mathsf{err}\}
and
h=h^{\prime}
, from (H2) and the definition of the satisfiability relation, it follows that
\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\notin}\mathsf{Val}
and by applying the same big-step semantics rule as in the OX case, we derive the second starjunct of our goal as required.

Lookup-err-use-after-free.
The Lookup-err-use-after-free rule is

\frac{\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq[``\mathsf{UseAfterFree}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E}),\mathsf{E}]}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{lookup-err-use-after-free}}

where
Q_{\mathit{err}}=\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{err}=\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}
.
To prove OX-soundness, the hypothese are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing

(H3)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

It then suffices to show that
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{err}=\mathit{E}_{\mathit{err}}
.
Given (H2), we can infer that
h(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s,h})=\varnothing
. From this we can infer that the only rule from the big-step operational semantics that can apply is:

({s},{h}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[{E}]\Downarrow_{\gamma}{\mathit{err}}:({s_{\mathit{err}}},{h})\begin{array}[]{c}\llbracket{{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=n\quad h(n)=\varnothing\\
v_{\mathit{err}}=[``\mathsf{UseAfterFree}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E}),n]\end{array}

From this, we can infer that
h^{\prime\prime}=h
and
s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{err}\mapsto v_{\mathit{err}}]
.
From (H2) and the definition of the satisfiability relation, it then follows that

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}
as required. For UX-soundness, our hypotheses are:

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}

(H3)

h_{f}~\sharp~h^{\prime}

It then suffices to show that for some
s,h
:

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})))

Letting
s=s^{\prime}\setminus\{\mathsf{err}\}
and
h=h^{\prime}
, from (H2) and the definition of the satisfiability relation, it follows that
\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing
and by applying the same big-step semantics rule as in the OX case, we derive the second conjunct of our goal as required.

New.
The New rule is

\frac{\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})\quad\theta\triangleq[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{new}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathop{\mbox{\Large$\varoast$}}{{}_{0\leq i<\mathsf{E}\theta}}((\mathsf{x}+i)\mapsto\mathtt{null})\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{new}}

For the OX-soundness, our hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})

(H3)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{new}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathop{\mbox{\Large$\varoast$}}{{}_{0\leq i<\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}}((\mathsf{x}+i)\mapsto\mathtt{null})\end{array})}

(H4)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}

(H5)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{new}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(H5) implies:

(H6)

o\neq\mathit{miss}

(H7)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=n

(H8)

\forall i\in\{0,...,n-1\}\ldotp n^{\prime}+i\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(h\uplus h_{f})

(H9)

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{x}\rightarrow n^{\prime}]

(H10)

h^{\prime\prime}=(h\uplus h_{f})[n^{\prime}\mapsto\mathtt{null}]\ldots[n^{\prime}+n-1\mapsto\mathtt{null}]

Defining
(H11)

h^{\prime}=h[n^{\prime}\mapsto\mathtt{null}]\ldots[n^{\prime}+n-1\mapsto\mathtt{null}]
yields with (H10) that
(H12)

h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}
.
(H2), (H4) and (H9) implies
(H13)

\theta,s^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=n^{\prime}
and (H11) then implies
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathop{\mbox{\Large$\varoast$}}{{}_{0\leq i<\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}}(\mathsf{x}+i)\mapsto\mathtt{null}
, which is the desired result.

For the UX direction, the hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\mathsf{x}\notin\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})

(H3)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{new}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathop{\mbox{\Large$\varoast$}}{{}_{0\leq i<\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}}((\mathsf{x}+i)\mapsto\mathtt{null})\end{array})}

(H4)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathop{\mbox{\Large$\varoast$}}{{}_{0\leq i<\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}}((\mathsf{x}+i)\mapsto\mathtt{null})

(H5)

h^{\prime}~\sharp~h_{f}

(H4) implies that
(H5)

\mathsf{x}\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(s^{\prime})
and we define

(H6)

n^{\prime}=s^{\prime}(\mathsf{x})

(H7)

n=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}[\mathsf{E}^{\prime}/\mathsf{x}]}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}

(H8)

h=h^{\prime}|_{d}
, where
d=\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(h^{\prime})\backslash\{s(\mathsf{x}),\ldots,s(\mathsf{x}+n)\}

(H9)

s=s^{\prime}[\mathsf{x}\rightarrow v]
where
v=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}

(H5) and (H8) impliy that
(H10)

n^{\prime}+i\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(h\uplus h_{f})\forall i\in\{0,...,n-1\}
and
(H11)

h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}=(h\uplus h_{f})[n^{\prime}\mapsto\mathtt{null}]\ldots[n^{\prime}+n-1\mapsto\mathtt{null}]
and (H9) implies
(H12)

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{x}\rightarrow n^{\prime}]
.
(H2), (H7) and (H9) imply
(H13)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}=n
.
(H10),(H11),(H12) and (H13)imply

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{new}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(H2) and (H9) imply
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=v
, which with (H7) and (H13) implies
\theta,s\models\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}
.

(H9), (H10), (H11) and (H13) imply the desired result.

Free.
The free rule is

\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\end{array})}\ {\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\end{array})}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{free}}

For OX-Soundness, the hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\end{array})}\ {\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\end{array})}

(H3)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}^{\prime}

(H4)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(H4) implies

(H5)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=n

(H6)

(h\uplus h_{f})(n)\in\mathsf{Val}

(H7)

s=s^{\prime}

(H8)

h^{\prime\prime}=(h\uplus h_{f})[n\mapsto\varnothing]

(H4) and (H5) impliy that
n\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(h)
, which with (H8) implies that
h^{\prime\prime}=h[n\mapsto\varnothing]\uplus h_{f}
. (H3) and (H7) imply
\theta,s^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}
.
(H5) and (H7) imply
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s^{\prime}}=n
and
defining
h^{\prime}=h[n\mapsto\varnothing]
, we obtain
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing
, which is the desired result.

For the UX direction, our hypotheses are

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\end{array})}\ {\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing\end{array})}

(H3)

\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{E}\mapsto\varnothing

(H4)

h^{\prime}~\sharp~h_{f}

Defining
s=s^{\prime}
, (H3) yields that
n=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Defining
h=h^{\prime}[n\mapsto v]
for
v=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s^{\prime}}=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}
, we obtain
h^{\prime}=h[n\mapsto\varnothing]
and therefore
h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}=(h\uplus h_{f})[n\mapsto\varnothing]
. The operational semantics of
\mathtt{free}
then yields

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})

and also obtain
\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{E}\mapsto\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
, which is the desired result.

∎

Appendix D
Basics of Scott Induction

The second soundness statement that needs to be proven for ESL is that well-formed environments are valid. This requires reasoning about the use of function specifications in the context of the environment extension rule.

In particular, the use of specifications of non-recursive functions is trivially sound. For recursive functions that always terminate, soundness can be proven by transfinite induction, while establishing a measure on the function pre-conditions and allowing recursive use of specifications only if they have a strictly lower measure. Without this requirement, we could prove an unsound specification
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\sf emp}\end{array})}\ {\mathtt{f}()}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{ret}=42\end{array})}
for the function
\mathtt{f}()\{\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{f}();\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{x}\}
, which does not hold since
\mathtt{f}
never terminates and the (satisfiable) post-condition
\mathsf{ret}=42
implies the existence of at least one terminating execution. This soundness issue does not arise in over-approximating logics, since, due to the meaning of triples, a satisfiable post-condition does not imply the existence of terminating traces. In these logics, it is always sound to apply a specification to prove itself.

However,
for recursive functions with non-terminating branches due to infinite recursion, we also have to be able to allow recursive use of specifications whose measure does not decrease, and the tool to handle such use is a form of fixpoint induction called Scott induction (see, e.g., Winskel
[
30
]
), which would normally be the tool for also proving soundness of well-formed environments in over-approximating logics.

In the following, we give the relevant Scott-induction-related definitions (from
[
30
]
), together with an instantiation that will be applied to prove soundness of well-formed environments in Appendix
E
.

Definition 21

(Domain)
.

A partially ordered set
(D,\sqsubseteq)
is a domain, iff

(D1)

\exists\bot\in D\ldotp\forall d\in D\ldotp\bot\sqsubseteq d
(least element)

(D2)

\forall(d_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq D\ldotp(\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp d_{i}\sqsubseteq d_{i+1})~\implies~\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}d_{n}\in D
(chain-closedness)

where
\sqcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}d_{n}
denotes the least upper bound or the supremum of the set
\{d_{n}~|~n\in\mathbb{N}\}
with respect to
\sqsubseteq
.

Definition 22

(Admissible Subset)
.

Given a domain
(D,\sqsubseteq)
with least element
\bot
, a subset
s\subseteq D
is called admissible, iff

(S1)

\bot\in s
(least element)

(S2)

\forall(s_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq s\ldotp(\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp s_{i}\sqsubseteq s_{i+1})~\implies~\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}s_{n}\in s
(chain-closedness)

Definition 23

(Continuity on Domains)
.

Assuming two domains
(D,\sqsubseteq_{D})
and
(\mathsf{E},\sqsubseteq_{\mathsf{E}})
, a function
g:D\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}
is continuous, iff

(C1)

\forall d,d^{\prime}\in D\ldotp d\sqsubseteq_{D}d^{\prime}\Longrightarrow g(d)\sqsubseteq_{\mathsf{E}}g(d^{\prime})
(monotonicity)

(C2)

\forall(d_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\ldotp(\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp d_{i}\sqsubseteq d_{i+1})~\Rightarrow~\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}g(d_{n})=g(\bigsqcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}d_{n})
(supremum-preservation)

Theorem 24

(Least fixpoint)
.

Given a domain
D
and a continuous function
g:D\longrightarrow D
, the least fixpoint of
g
, denoted by
\text{\it lfp}(g)
, has the identity

\text{\it lfp}(g)=\bigsqcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}g^{n}(\bot),

where
\bot
denotes the least element of
D
and
g^{n}
denotes the
n
-times application of
g
.

Theorem 25

(Scott Induction Principle)
.

Given a domain
D
, an admissible subset
s\subseteq D
, and a continuous function
g:D\longrightarrow D
, it holds that

g(s)\subseteq s~\Longrightarrow~\text{\it lfp}(g)\in s

Before presenting the instantiation of the Scott induction, we require a pseudo-command
\mathtt{scope}
which models the function call, and pseudo-commands for non-deterministic choice.

Definition 26

(The
\mathtt{scope}
pseudo-command)
.

We define a pseudo-command which closely models the behaviour of a function call:

\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C,~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))

whose arguments are

•

a pair
(\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}})
consisting of a list of distinct program variables and a list of expressions, such that both are of the same length,

•

a command
C
which is to be executed within the ”scope”,

•

a tuple
(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
of a program variable and an expression,

and whose semantics (eliding expression-evaluation fault cases) is given by

({s},{h}),\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C,~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]},{h^{\prime}})\begin{array}[]{c}\llbracket{\vec{{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\quad\mathtt{pv}({C})\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\{\vec{\mathsf{z}}\}\\
s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\quad(s_{p},h),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s_{q}},{h^{\prime}})\quad\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\end{array}

({s},{h}),\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C,~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma}err:(s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow v_{err}],h^{\prime})\begin{array}[]{c}\llbracket{\vec{{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\quad\mathtt{pv}({C})\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\{\vec{\mathsf{z}}\}\\
s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\quad(s_{p},h),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}err:(s_{q},h^{\prime})\quad\llbracket{\mathsf{err}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v_{err}\end{array}

Definition 27

(Non-Deterministic Choice (pseudo-commands))
.

We furthermore add pseudo-commands that arbitrarily pick a command form a given set and executes it:

\sigma,C_{1}\sqcup C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\lor\sigma,C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\quad\sigma,\bigsqcup(C_{n}~|~n\in\mathbb{N})\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\exists m\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\sigma,C_{m}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

We will now give some general definitions and lemmas, which will later on be instantiated to prove the soundness of the environment extension via Theorem
25
.

Definition 28

(Greatest-Fixpoint Closure of
\mathsf{Cmd}
)
.

We define the greatest-fixpoint closure of the set of commands and pseudo-commands,
\mathsf{Cmd}\cup\{\mathtt{scope},\sqcup,\bigsqcup\}
, as the closure of that set under infinite applications of the command constructors, and denote that closure by
\mathbb{C}
.

Definition 29

(Behavioural Equivalence on
\mathbb{C}
)
.

Given an arbitrary function implementation context
\gamma
, we define the equivalence relation
\simeq_{\gamma}
on
\mathbb{C}
as

C_{1}\simeq_{\gamma}C_{2}\iff\{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}^{2}~|~\exists o\ldotp~\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}=\{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}^{2}~|~\exists o\ldotp~\sigma,C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}

where
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, effectively meaning that
\simeq_{\gamma}
relates commands that exhibit the same set of behaviours. We denote the resulting quotient space as
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
and the corresponding equivalence class of a command
C
by
[C]
.
This relation yields a partial order, denoted by
\sqsubseteq_{\gamma}
and defined as:

C_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma}C_{2}\iff\{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}^{2}~|~\exists o\ldotp~\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}\subseteq\{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}^{2}~|~\exists o\ldotp~\sigma,C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}

Furthermore, we define the join operator on commands in
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
,
\mathbin{{\sqcup}_{\gamma}}
, as the non-deterministic choice,
lift it to quotient space, overloading notation:

[C_{1}]\sqcup[C_{2}]=[C_{1}\sqcup C_{2}]

and generalise it to countably infinitely many commands/equivalence classes in the standard way.

The relation
\simeq_{\gamma}
is an equivalence relation as it inherits reflexivity, symmetry and transitivity from the equality relation on sets, and

\sqsubseteq_{\gamma}
is a partial order on
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
as it inherits transitivity and reflexivity from set inclusion, while
\simeq_{\gamma}
ensures anti-symmetry.

Furthermore, note that, by design of the language, we do not have to bring the outcome
o
into the equivalence relation, as faulting states can be distinguished from successful ones by having the dedicated program variable
\mathsf{err}
in the store, and language errors can be distinguished from the missing resource errors by the value that
\mathsf{err}
holds.

Lemma 30

(Domain Property)
.

For any function implementation context
\gamma
,
(\mathbb{C}_{\gamma},\sqsubseteq_{\gamma})
is a domain.

Proof.

Since we have already argued the partial order property, there are only remaining two properties to show:

Chain-Closedness.

For any chain
([C_{n}])_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
, its supremum is defined as
[\sqcup(C_{n}|_{n\in\mathbb{N}})]
. Per definition of
\mathbb{C}
we have
\sqcup(C_{n}|_{n\in\mathbb{N}})\in\mathbb{C}
which implies
[\sqcup(C_{n}|_{n\in\mathbb{N}})]\in\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
.

Least Element.

The least element of
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
, denoted by
\bot_{\gamma}
, is the equivalence class of commands which, given the function implementation context
\gamma
, do not terminate on any state. One such representative is the command
C=\mathtt{while}\ ({\sf true})\ \mathtt{skip}
. Since
\{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}^{2}~|~\sigma,\mathtt{while}\ ({\sf true})\ \mathtt{skip}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\}=\emptyset
, we trivially obtain
\bot_{\gamma}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma}[C]
, for all
[C]\in\mathbb{C}_{\gamma}
.
∎

Lemma 31

(Scope and Function Call Equivalence)
.

Given a function implementation context
\gamma
and a function
f
such that
\gamma(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
, it holds that

\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{f},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\simeq_{\gamma}\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})

Proof.

We show in detail the case of successful execution; the faulting cases are analogous.
Let
\gamma(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
and
(s,h),(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}
, such that

(s,h),\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{f},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

The operational semantics of
\mathtt{scope}
implies:

•

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]

•

\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}

•

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}

•

s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]

•

(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})

Due to the assumption
\gamma(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
, we fulfil all conditions in the antecedent of the operational semantics of the function call, and therefore obtain
(s,h),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

Now, let
(s,h),(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\mathsf{State}
such that
(s,h),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. The operational semantics of the function call implies:

•

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]

•

\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}

•

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}

•

s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]

•

(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})

•

\gamma(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})

Therefore, we fulfil all conditions in the antecedent of the operational semantics of
\mathtt{scope}
, and therefore obtain

(s,h),\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{f},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. ∎

In the following, let
C_{i}
denote the implementation of the function
f_{i}
, and
C^{i}
denote the
i
-th component of a vector
C
.

Definition 32

(Function Call Substitution)
.

Given a command
\bar{C}\in\mathsf{Cmd}
, a vector of
n
commands
C=(C^{1},\ldots,C^{n})\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, a vector of
n
functions
F=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})
and a function implementation context
\gamma
, such that
F\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)
, we define a function call substitution
\bar{C}[C,\gamma,F]
recursively on the structure of
\bar{C}
, with
\gamma(f_{i})=(\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},-,\mathsf{E}_{i})
:

•

(\mathtt{if}\ (B)\ C_{1}\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{2})[C,\gamma,F]:=\mathtt{if}\ (B)\ \{C_{1}[C,\gamma,F]\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{C_{2}[C,\gamma,F]\}

•

(\mathtt{while}\ (B)\ \bar{C})[C,\gamma,F]:=\mathtt{while}\ (B)\ \{\bar{C}[C,\gamma,F]\}

•

(C_{1};C_{2})[C,\gamma,F]:=C_{1}[C,\gamma,F];C_{2}[C,\gamma,F]

•

(\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{g}(\vec{\mathsf{E}}))[C,\gamma,F]:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C^{i},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}_{i}))&\text{if }f_{i}=\mathtt{g}\\
\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{g}(\vec{\mathsf{E}})&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

•

\bar{C}[C,\gamma,F]:=\bar{C}
, for all other
\bar{C}
.

Lemma 33

(Substitution Preserves
\simeq_{\gamma}
)
.

Given
I=\{1,...,n\}
,
F=(f_{1},...,f_{n})
, and
\gamma
such that
\forall i\in I\ldotp\gamma(f_{i})=(-,C_{i},-)
,
C=(C_{1},...,C_{n})
, and
i\in I
, it holds that

C_{i}\simeq_{\gamma}C_{i}[C,\gamma,F]

Proof.

We prove the statement by structural induction on
C_{i}
. Per definition of function implementation contexts,
C_{i}\in\mathsf{Cmd}
and is therefore finite. The only non-trivial cases are the structural commands and the function call on
f_{i}
, as the substitution is the identity otherwise.

If-Else.

C_{i}=\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}
. Let
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that
\sigma,\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
, and let
\sigma=(s,h)
.
Then, the operational semantics implies

\begin{array}[]{l l}&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{t}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\big)\lor\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}={\mathtt{false}}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{f}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\big)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\Leftrightarrow}}&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{t}}[C,\gamma,F]\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\big)\lor\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}={\mathtt{false}}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{f}}[C,\gamma,F]\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\big)\end{array}

which is equivalent to

(s,h),\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}[C,\gamma,F]\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}[C,\gamma,F]\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
. The faulting case is proven analogously to the successful case, and the while loop and the sequencing are proven analogously to if-else.

Function Call.
We have to show that

(\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f_{i}(\vec{\mathsf{E}}))\simeq_{\gamma}\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{i},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}_{i}))

where
\gamma(fi)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},C_{i},\mathsf{E}_{i}),
but this holds directly due to Lemma
31
.
∎

Before moving on to the Scott instantiation, we define a notion of (recursion) depth, which keeps track of the maximum number of nested function calls during the execution of commands.

Definition 34

(Depth)
.

Given a command
C\in\mathsf{Cmd}
, a vector of functions
F=(f_{1},...,f_{n})
and a derivation
\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}
, we define
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})
inductively on the structure of big-step derivations of
C
as follows, noting that we extend the notion of a maximal element to the empty set by defining it to be zero:

If-Else.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})~\triangleq\\
\qquad\max\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),C_{\mathtt{t}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\mid\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\},\\
\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),C_{\mathtt{f}}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\mid\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{false}\}\\
\end{array}\right)\end{array}

Sequence.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{1};C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\triangleq\\
\qquad\max\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\bar{\sigma}),~\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\bar{\sigma},C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\mid\\
\qquad\qquad\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\bar{\sigma}\land\bar{\sigma},C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\},\\
\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\mid o=\mathit{err}/\mathit{miss}\land\sigma,C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}\end{array}\right)\end{array}

While.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})~\triangleq\\
\qquad\max\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}\bar{\sigma}),~\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\bar{\sigma},\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})~|\\
\qquad\qquad\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}\bar{\sigma}\land\bar{\sigma},\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\},\\
\max\{\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\mid o=\mathit{err}/\mathit{miss}\land\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\}\end{array}\right)\end{array}

Function Call.

\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{g}(\vec{\mathsf{E}}),\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\triangleq 0,\quad\text{if $\forall i\in I\ldotp\mathtt{g}\neq f_{i}$}

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s,h),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f_{i}(\vec{\mathsf{E}}),\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\triangleq\\
\qquad\max\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\max\{1+\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s_{p},h),C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime}))\mid\\
\qquad o=\mathit{ok},\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v},(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime}),\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{i}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\},\\
\max\{1+\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s_{p},h),C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s_{q},h^{\prime}))\mid\\
\qquad o=\mathit{err}/\mathit{miss},\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v},(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s_{q},h^{\prime})\},\\
\max\{1+\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s_{p},h),C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s_{q},h^{\prime}))\mid\\
\qquad o=\mathit{err},\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v},(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime}),\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{i}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=\lightning\},\\
\end{array}\right)\end{array}

where
\gamma(f_{i})=(\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},C_{i},\mathsf{E}_{i})
, and
s_{p}
and
s^{\prime}
are defined as in the operational semantics of the function call.

Remaining Commands.

\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime})\triangleq 0
.

D.1
1-dimensional Scott Instantiation

The env-extend rule allows us to add a set of
n
functions to a given valid environment.
Soundness of this rule is proven in Appendix
E
through transfinite induction. In each of the cases (zero, successor ordinal, limit ordinal), a Scott induction is required.
In §
D.2
, we present and prove the Scott induction required to show soundness of the env-extend rule.
Here, we present the Scott induction as required for the case where only
one
function is added to a given environment at a time.

The general proof, as presented in §
D.2
evolves naturally from and relies heavily on this simpler case, while introducing heavier notation.
To minimize clutter in later definitions, we introduce the over-approximation quadruple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}
and define its notion of validity.

Definition 35

(OX-Validity)
.

Given an OX-quadruple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}
and a function we define for an arbitrary implementation context
\gamma

\begin{array}[]{l}\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\Longleftrightarrow\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\forall\theta,s,h,o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime},h_{f}\ldotp\theta,s,h\models P\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\quad\quad~\Longrightarrow~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\quad\quad\quad~\Longrightarrow~o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}\end{array}

and for an arbitrary specification context
\Gamma

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\Longleftrightarrow\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\forall\gamma\ldotp\models(\gamma,\Gamma)\Longrightarrow\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\end{array}

In the following, we will also write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}
as a shorthand for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}
. Onward, we assume the following:

(A1)

a valid environment,
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
;

(A2)

a function
f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{C_{f};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}^{\prime}\}
that is not in the domain of
\gamma
;

(A3)

an arbitrary element
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
;

(A4)

a set of terminating (external) specifications for
f
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta<\alpha\}
;

(A5)

a set of non-terminating (external) specifications for
f
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta\leq\alpha\}
;

(A6)

an extension of
\gamma
with
f
:
\gamma^{\prime}\triangleq\gamma[f\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})]
; and

(A7)

an extension of
\Gamma
with the given specifications of
f
:

\Gamma(\alpha)\triangleq\Gamma[f\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta<\alpha\}\cup\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta\leq\alpha\})]

We next define the function
g:\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, to be used in the upcoming Scott induction, as follows:

g([C])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{\small{def}}}}{{=}}[h(C)]

where
h:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is defined as
h(C):=C_{f}[C,\gamma^{\prime},f]
.

Intuitively,
h
takes an arbitrary command
C
from
\mathbb{C}
as an argument and substitutes it for any function call on
f
in function body
C_{f}
.
The function
g
then lifts this operation to the quotient space
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
.
The definitions of
h
and
g
trivially yield the following identities for arbitrary
C\in\mathbb{C}
and
(C_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
:

(G1)

\mathop{\bigsqcup{\!}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}g([C_{n}])=[\mathop{\bigsqcup{\!}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h(C_{n})]

(G2)

\mathop{\bigsqcup{\!}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}g^{n}([C])=[\mathop{\bigsqcup{\!}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h^{n}(C)]

Lemma 36
.

The function
g
is continuous.

Proof.

We begin by proving monotonicity.

Monotonicity.

We prove the monotonicity of
h
. We need to show that for all
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, it holds that

C_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{2}\Rightarrow C_{f}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{f}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]

Let
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that
\sigma,C_{f}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}
. We need to show that

\sigma,C_{f}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}

and we do so by structural induction on
C_{f}
.
The only non-trivial cases are the compound commands and the function call, as the substitution is the identity for all other cases.

If-Else.

C_{f}=\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}
. Let
(s,h),\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that

(s,h),\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}

The operational semantics yields (for an appropriate
\sigma{\mathit{err}}
):

\begin{array}[]{l l}&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{t}}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\big)~\lor\\
&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}={\mathtt{false}}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{f}}[C_{1},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\big)~\lor\\
&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\lightning\land\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma{\mathit{err}}\big)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\Rightarrow}}&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\mathtt{true}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{t}}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\big)~\lor\\
&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}={\mathtt{false}}\land(s,h),C_{\mathtt{f}}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}\big)~\lor\\
&\big(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\lightning\land\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma{\mathit{err}}\big)\\
\end{array}

which implies the desired

(s,h),\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C_{\mathtt{t}}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]\ \mathtt{else}\ C_{\mathtt{f}}[C_{2},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}

The while loop and the sequencing cases are proven analogously.

Function Call.

C_{f}=\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})
. Using Lemma
31
and considering the successful case only as the faulting cases are proven analogously, let
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that

\sigma,\mathtt{scope}((\mathsf{x},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{1},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}

Letting
\sigma=(s,h)
and
\sigma^{\prime}=(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, the operational semantics for
\mathtt{scope}
then implies

\begin{array}[]{l l}&s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]\land\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\land\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\land\mathtt{pv}(C)\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\vec{\mathsf{z}}\\
&\land~s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\land(s_{p},h),C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle C_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{2}}}{{\implies}}&s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]\land\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\land\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\land\mathtt{pv}(C)\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\vec{\mathsf{z}}\\
&\land~s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\land(s_{p},h),C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})\\
\end{array}

which implies the desired

\sigma,\mathtt{scope}((\mathsf{x},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C_{2},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}

The monotonicity of
g
follows straightforwardly from the monotonicity of
h
.

Supremum-Preservation.

Assume a chain
(C_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathbb{C}
.
First, we show that

\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h(C_{n})\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}h\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n}\big)
:

\begin{array}[]{r l}&\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h(C_{n})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\exists m\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\sigma,h(C_{m})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\exists m\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\sigma,C_{f}[C_{m},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,C_{f}[\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,h\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n}\big)\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}

Next, we show that
h\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n}\big)\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h(C_{n})
.
Let
\sigma,h\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n}\big)\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
,

i.e.

\sigma,C_{f}[\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{n},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
Then, since
C_{f}\in\mathsf{Cmd}
, it is a finite command and hence has a finite number
t
of function calls on
f
. At each function call substitution site, the execution will execute some command
C_{n}
. Assume
k_{1},...,k_{t}\in\mathbb{N}
such that at the
i
-th execution site, the command
C_{k_{i}}
is executed, and let
k\triangleq\max{(k_{1},...,k_{t})}
. Since
(C_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a chain, we have that
C_{k_{i}}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{k}
for all
i\in\{1,...,t\}
and therefore:

\begin{array}[]{r l}&\sigma,C_{f}[C_{k},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_{f}[C_{n},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h(C_{n})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}

The supremum preservation of
g
follows trivially from the supremum preservation of
h
.
∎

Next, we introduce the admissible set we will use in this instantiation of the Scott induction.

Lemma 37

(Admissible Subset
S^{\alpha}
)
.

The set
S^{\alpha}
, defined as

\begin{array}[]{l}S^{\alpha}:=\{[\bar{C}]\in\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}~|~\exists C\in[\bar{C}]\ldotp\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)\ldotp\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)\ldotp\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\}\end{array}

is an admissible subset of
(\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}},\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

Least Element.

We know that
\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}}=[\mathtt{while}\ (\mathtt{true})\ \mathtt{skip}]
and that this commands trivially semantically satisfies any OX-quadruple. Therefore,
\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}}\in S^{\alpha}
.

Chain-Closure.

We need to show that given an arbitrary chain
([C^{\prime}_{n}])_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq S^{\alpha}
, it holds that
\bigsqcup([C^{\prime}_{n}]~|~n\in\mathbb{N})\in S^{\alpha}
. Onwards, we will use the following notation:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{n}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}\end{array}\right)}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{n}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}\end{array}\right)}

The definition of
S^{\alpha}
yields the existence of a chain
(C_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds that
C_{n}\in[C^{\prime}_{n}]
and

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)}\in((\Gamma(\alpha))(f)\ldotp\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{n}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{n}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C_{n}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}\end{array}\right\}}

Per definition of
\mathit{Int}
, we know that
P_{n}=P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Together with the definition of choice, we obtain that

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\bigsqcup(C_{n}|n\in\mathbb{N})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}\end{array}\right\}}

It remains to show that
(P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null})~(\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{n})
is an internalisation of
(P)~({\mathcal{Q}})
. Since
(P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null})~({\mathcal{Q}}_{n})
are internalisations of
(P)~({\mathcal{Q}})
, we obtain

\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp(Q_{\mathit{ok}}~\Leftrightarrow~\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}])\land(Q_{\mathit{err}}~\Leftrightarrow~\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}])

This implies

\begin{array}[]{r l}Q_{\mathit{ok}}~\Leftrightarrow&\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\big)\\
~\Leftrightarrow&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\big)\\
