~\Leftrightarrow&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\big(\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\big)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\\
~\Leftrightarrow&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\big(\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{n}\big)[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\end{array}

and analogously

\begin{array}[]{r l}Q_{\mathit{err}}~\Leftrightarrow&\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\big)\\
~\Leftrightarrow&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\big)\\
~\Leftrightarrow&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\big(\bigvee_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{n}\big)[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\end{array}

Therefore,
[\sqcup(C_{n}|n\in\mathbb{N})]\in S^{\alpha}
, which yields
\sqcup([C_{n}]|n\in\mathbb{N})\in S^{\alpha}
and finally
\sqcup([C^{\prime}_{n}]|n\in\mathbb{N})\in S^{\alpha}
.
∎

This concludes the set-up for Scott induction, which allows us to prove the inductive step.

Lemma 38

(Scott Condition)
.

Under the assumptions (A1)-(A7) and additionally assuming

\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)\ldotp\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{f}:t^{\prime}

(1)

and

\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{f}:t^{\prime}

(2)

it holds that
g(S^{\alpha})\subseteq S^{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Let
[C]\in S^{\alpha}
. Therefore, there exists a
C^{\prime}\in[C]
such that for all
t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)
exists a
(P)~({\mathcal{Q}})\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)
such that
\textbf{(H)}~\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}
. This implies that
C^{\prime}
does not call on
f
, because
\Gamma
holds no specifications for
f
. Hence,
C_{f}[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]
does not call on
f
either.
We prove the statement by showing the more general claim

{\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}}\implies\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C_{f}[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}

for arbitrary precondition
P
and postcondition
{\mathcal{Q}}
by induction over the structure of
C_{f}
.

Base Commands and Function Calls on
g\not=f
.
In this case,
C_{f}[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]=C_{f}
and
C_{f}
does not call on
f
.
Therefore, the proof tree of
\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}
uses no specifications on
f
, which implies that
\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}
. From
\text{(A1)}~\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
, we obtain
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}
, which implies
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}
.

Compound Command Rules: if-then, if-else, sequence.

If-then, if-else and sequence are proven directly, using the IH.

Compound Command Rules: while.
The following proof is based on a while rule
while-iterate
, which is a while rule from an earlier version of our proof rules. In the current version of the proof rules, we have simplified the
while-iterate
rule to the
while
rule as introduced in the main body. As the earlier
while-iterate
rule encompasses the simplified
while
rule, we have keep the proof here as is.
Given the rule

\frac{\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}\qquad P_{\infty}\triangleq\mathsf{False}\quad\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\land\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array}\right)}\quad\quad m\triangleq\min(\{i\in\mathbb{N}\uplus\{\infty\}\mid~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow\lnot\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}\})}{\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{0}\end{array}\right)}\ {\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{m}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists n<m\ldotp Q_{n}\end{array}\right)}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{while-iterate}}

we assume

(W1)

\theta,s,h\models P_{0}

(W2)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\bar{\gamma}}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(W3)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{E}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}

(W4)

P_{\infty}\triangleq\mathsf{False}

(W5)

\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp~\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\land\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right)}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array}\right)}

(W6)

m\triangleq\min(\{i\in\mathbb{N}\uplus\{\infty\}\mid~\models P_{i}\Rightarrow\lnot\mathsf{E}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}\})

Noting that
(\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C)[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]=\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]
,
(W2) implies that

(W7)

(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s,h\uplus h_{f}}=\mathtt{false}\land(s,h\uplus h_{f})=(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}))\lor\\
(\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s,h\uplus h_{f}}=\mathtt{true}\land(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{\sigma}\land\bar{\sigma},\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}))

and (W5) and the inductive hypothesis imply

\textbf{(W8)}\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\ldotp\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\land\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i+1}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i}\end{array}\right\}}

We know that
m\neq\infty
, as otherwise the loop would be non-terminating, contradicting (W2).
If
m=0
, then
\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}=\mathtt{false}
, and (W7) trivially yields the desired result.

Otherwise, we have that
m>0
. Then,
(W8) yields

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i-1}\land\mathsf{E}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{i}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{i-1}\end{array}\right\}}

for all
0<i\leq m
and (W6) yields
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{m}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{m}\end{array}\right\}}
.
From here, applying the operational semantics of while
m
times, similarly to the proof in RHL
[
8
]
, we obtain the desired

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{0}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathtt{while}\ (\mathsf{E})\ C[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{m}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists n<m\ldotp Q_{n}\end{array}\right\}}

Structural rules.

All four structural rules (equiv, exists, frame, and disj) are proven trivially, using the inductive hypothesis. We give the proof for the equivalence rule:

\frac{\models P\Leftrightarrow P^{\prime}\quad\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\quad\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow Q_{\mathit{ok}}\quad\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow Q_{\mathit{err}}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right)}}\quad\textsc{\mbox{equiv}}

where the IH gives us
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C_{f}[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}
, from which the desired claim is obtained trivially.

Function call on
f
.

(\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{f}(\vec{\mathsf{E}}))[C^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},f]=\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C^{\prime},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))
, where
\gamma^{\prime}(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
. Therefore, we need to show that

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C^{\prime},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}

The assumption
\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{f}(\vec{\mathsf{E}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}
implies via the function call rule that
P=(\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P^{*})
, where
P^{*}
is the program-variable-free part either the pre-condition
P_{\infty}
of the non-terminating or the pre-condition
P
of the (partially) terminating specification, and that
(\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P^{*})~({\mathcal{Q}})\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)
.
We assume the following:

(F0)

an arbitrary
\bar{\gamma}
such that
\models(\bar{\gamma},\Gamma(\alpha))

(F1)

\theta,s,h\models\mathsf{y}=\mathit{E}_{y}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{E}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P^{*}

(F2)

arbitrary
h_{f}
and
h^{\prime\prime}
such that
(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathtt{scope}((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}}),~C^{\prime},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))\Downarrow_{\bar{\gamma}}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

We need to show that

(o\neq\mathit{miss})\land\big(\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land((o=ok\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}})\lor(o=err\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}))\big)

Defining

(F3)

\vec{v}:=\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}

(F4)

\vec{\mathsf{z}}:=\mathtt{pv}(C^{\prime})\backslash\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}
.

(F5)

s_{p}:=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]
,

the operational semantics of
\mathtt{scope}
and (F2) imply

(F6)

(s_{p},h\uplus h_{f}),C^{\prime}\Downarrow_{\bar{\gamma}}o:(s_{q},h^{\prime\prime})

(F7a)

o=ok\Rightarrow(s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}])

(F7b)

o=err\Rightarrow(s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow\llbracket{\mathsf{err}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}])

The definition of
S^{\alpha}
implies existence of a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{*}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)})
such that
(H8a)

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}
. (A1) and (F0) imply
\models(\bar{\gamma},\Gamma)
, which yields with (H8a) that
(H8b)

\bar{\gamma}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {C^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}

Per definition of the internalisation, we know that
P^{\prime}=\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P^{*}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
, which implies with (F3)-(F5) that
(H9)

\theta,s_{p},h\models P^{\prime}
.
Then,
(F6), (F8b) and (F9) imply

\textbf{(H10)}~~(o\neq\mathit{miss})\land\big(\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land((o=ok\land\theta,s_{q},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime})\lor(o=err\land\theta,s_{q},h^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}))\big)

This yields
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q_{o}
as
Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}
and
Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}
are internalisations of
Q_{\mathit{ok}}
and
Q_{\mathit{err}}
respectively, which in turn implies the desired result, i.e.

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathtt{scope}(((\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{E}})),~C^{\prime},~(\mathsf{y},\mathsf{E}^{\prime}))}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}

This concludes the proof of the general statement. Instantiating it with the existentially quantified
t^{\prime}
from (
1
) and (
2
) then yields the desired result

[C]\in S^{\alpha}~\Longrightarrow~[h(C)]\in S^{\alpha}

i.e., the Scott condition
g(S^{\alpha})\subseteq S^{\alpha}
.

∎

Finally, we need to show that the function body
C_{f}
of
f
is indeed equivalent to the least fixpoint of
g
, denoted by
\text{\it lfp}(g)
.

Lemma 39

(Scott’s last step)
.

The function body
C_{f}
is in the least fixpoint of
g
, i.e.

C_{f}\in\text{\it lfp}(g)

Again, we prove a slightly different statement first, and then apply it to prove the lemma. Onward, we write
\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}}
to denote the least element of
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
(and also of
S^{\alpha}
).
Onwards, we will use
\mathtt{wts}
as a shorthand for the command
\mathtt{while}\ (\mathtt{true})\ \mathtt{skip}
. Keep in mind that
\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}}=[\mathtt{wts}]
.

Lemma 40
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds that

\forall\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}\ldotp~\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}~\land~\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})\leq n\iff\sigma,h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}

where do not explicitly include the outcome statement
o
to avoid clutter.

Proof.

By induction on
n
.

Base Case:
n=0
.

”
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}~\land~\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})=0
. Since
C_{f}\in\mathsf{Cmd}
, it cannot include the
\mathtt{scope}
command. Since the depth is zero, the execution path does not reach a function call on
f
. Hence, this call may be replaced by any other command, including
\mathtt{wts}
, i.e.

\begin{array}[]{c l}&\sigma,C_{f}[\mathtt{wts},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\sigma,h(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

”
\Leftarrow
”: Assuming

\sigma,h(wts)\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
, we obtain
\sigma,C_{f}[\mathtt{wts},\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathtt{wts}
does not terminate on any state, this implies that the execution does not reach the command
\mathtt{wts}
, that is, it does not reach any function call site of
f
and, therefore, we obtain
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})=0
.

Inductive Step.
Assume that the equivalence holds for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and the goal is then to prove that it holds for
n+1
.

”
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}~\land~\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})\leq n+1
.
If
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})<n+1
, the inductive hypothesis yields
\sigma,h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
,
Since
h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})
implies that the execution terminates and therefore does not reach any instance of the command
\mathtt{wts}
, we may substitute
\mathtt{wts}
for any other command, including
h(\mathtt{wts})
. This yields
\sigma,h^{n+2}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Finally, let
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})=n+1
and let
\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})
be an arbitrary function call on
f
reached by the execution.
That means that there exist states
\sigma 1
and
\sigma 2
, such that the execution of the initial part of
C_{f}
from
\sigma
up to that function call yields
\sigma 1
, that
\sigma 1,\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma 2
, and that the execution of the remaining part of
C_{f}
on
\sigma 2
yields
\sigma^{\prime}
.
By instantiating the operational semantics of the function call with
\sigma 1
and
\sigma 2
, we obtain
(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s_{q},h^{\prime})
, where, per definition of the
\mathtt{depth}
function,
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s_{q},h^{\prime}))=n
.
The inductive hypothesis then implies
(s_{p},h),h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})
. Therefore,
\sigma,C_{f}[h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts}),\gamma^{\prime},\\
fid]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
and since this command does not call on
f
and given the definition of
h
, we obtain
\sigma,h^{n+2}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
.

”
\Leftarrow
”: Assume
\sigma,h^{n+2}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
: that is,
\sigma,C_{f}[h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts}),\gamma^{\prime},f]\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
.
If there are no substitution sites in
C_{f}
, the desired goal is obtained trivially, as the substitution is vacuous and the considered depth is zero by definition.
Otherwise, consider an arbitrary substitution site in
C_{f}
reached by the execution starting from
\sigma
, and let the state before the substitution site be some
\sigma 1
. By instantiating the operational semantics of
\mathtt{scope}
, we obtain
s_{p}
,
s_{q}
,
h^{\prime}
and
\sigma 2
, such that
(s_{p},h),h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s_{q},h^{\prime})
and the remaining part of
C_{f}[h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts}),\gamma^{\prime},f]
executed on
\sigma 2
terminates in
\sigma^{\prime}
.
Per the inductive hypothesis, we have that
(s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s_{q},h^{\prime})
and
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}((s_{p},h),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s_{q},h^{\prime}))\leq n
. This implies, given the operational semantics of function call and the definition of depth, that
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}(s_{q},h^{\prime})
and
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})\leq n+1
, which concludes the proof.
∎

With this in place, we can prove Lemma
39
, concluding the overall proof:

Proof of Lemma
39
.

We know that the least fixpoint of
g
obeys the following identity:

\text{\it lfp}(g)=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}g^{n}(\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}})=[\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h^{n}(\mathtt{wts})]

(3)

To prove the statement of the lemma, we will show that

\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h^{n}(\mathtt{wts})\simeq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{f}

”
\Rightarrow
”: Assume
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that
\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h^{n}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
. Therefore, there exists an
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sigma,h^{n}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
Since the command
\mathtt{wts}
does not terminate on any state and since
h^{0}(\mathtt{wts})=\mathtt{wts}
,
n
must be strictly positive. Lemma
40
then implies that
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
, which concludes the proof.

”
\Leftarrow
”: Assuming
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that
\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
, we know that
C_{f}
terminates when executed on
\sigma
, and therefore has a finite execution depth: that is,
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})=n
holds for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Lemma
40
then implies
\sigma,h^{n+1}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
, and therefore

\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}h^{n}(\mathtt{wts})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}
.

This yields
C_{f}\in\text{\it lfp}(g)
.
∎

Theorem
25
and
Lemmas
38
and
39
finally fnimply that
[C_{f}]\in S^{\alpha}
.

D.2
n-dimensional Scott Instantiation

The instantiation presented so far suffices to prove soundness for recursive functions which potentially have both terminating and non-terminating specifications. However, we wish to allow clusters of
mutually
recursive functions, and hence require a more general instantiation of the Scott induction.
In particular, given an environment
(\gamma,\Gamma)
, we add on a mutually recursive cluster
F:=(f_{1},...,f_{n})
of
n
functions.

We assume the following:

(B1)

a valid environment,
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
;

(B2)

a set of
n
functions
f_{i}(\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i})\{C_{i};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}_{i}\}
with
i\in I\triangleq\{1,...,n\}
that is not in the domain of
\gamma
;

(B3)

an arbitrary element
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
;

(B4)

a set of terminating (external) specifications for each
f_{i}
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta<\alpha\}
;

(B5)

a set of non-terminating (external) specifications for each
f_{i}
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta\leq\alpha\}
;

(B6)

an extension of
\gamma
:
\gamma^{\prime}\triangleq\gamma[f_{i}\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i},C_{i},\mathsf{E}_{i})]_{i\in I}
; and

(B7)

an extension of
\Gamma
with the given specifications:

\Gamma(\alpha)\triangleq\Gamma[f_{i}\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta<\alpha\}\cup\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}~|~\beta\leq\alpha\}]_{i\in I}

Note on Notation.
As most elements we will be using in this section are
n
-tuples of commands, or chains of such
n
-tuples, we use the following notation:

•

C_{i}
: a subscript
i
denotes that the commands
C_{i}
is the implementation of the function
f_{i}
, as recorded in the associated function implementation context
\gamma^{\prime}
,

•

C^{i}
:
a superscript
i
denotes the
i
-th component of an
n
-tuple
C\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,

•

C(i)
: an index
i
denotes the
i
-th element of a chain (monotonically increasing sequence)
\big(C(m)\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Lemma 41

(n-Dimensional Domain)
.

Given the domain
(\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}},\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}})
, we lift the equivalence relation, partial order, and the join operator of
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
to elements of
\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{n}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{l}C\simeq_{n}\bar{C}~\Longleftrightarrow~\forall i\in I\ldotp C^{i}\simeq_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{C}^{i}\\
C\sqsubseteq_{n}\bar{C}~\Longleftrightarrow~\forall i\in I\ldotp C^{i}\sqsubseteq_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{C}^{i}\\
C\sqcup_{n}\bar{C}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}C^{1}\sqcup_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{C}^{1}\\
\vdots\\
C^{n}\sqcup_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{C}^{n}\end{array}\right)\end{array}

The proof that these satisfy the appropriate properties is trivial with the least element
nle
being the equivalence class of the
n
-tuple which holds
\mathtt{while}\ (\mathtt{true})\ \mathtt{skip}
in every component.

Lemma 42

(n-Dimensional Continuous G)
.

The function
G:\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, defined as

G([C]):=[H(C)]

where

H(C):=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}(C)\\
\vdots\\
h_{n}(C)\end{array}\right)

and

h_{i}:\mathbb{C}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C},~h_{i}(C):=C_{i}[C,\gamma^{\prime},F],

is continuous.

Proof.

Again, what needs to be proven is monotonicity and supremum-preservation.

Monotonicity.
Analogously to the one-dimensional case, it is proven that
h_{i}
is monotonic for all
i\in I
. This implies that
H
and, therefore,
G
is monotonic as well.

Supremum-Preservation.
Given a chain
\big(C(m)\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
, we need to prove:

\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}G([C(m)])=G\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}[C(m)]\big)

Due to the definition of
G
, we obtain for the left-hand side

\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}G({[C(m)]})=[\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}H(C(m))]

and for the right-hand side

G\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}[C(m)]\big)=[H(\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}C(m))]

It therefore suffices to show the equivalence of
\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}H(C(m))
and
H(\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}C(m))
, i.e. for all
i\in I
:

\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}C_{i}[C(m),\gamma^{\prime},F]\simeq_{\gamma^{\prime}}C_{i}[~\sqcup(C(m)|m\in\mathbb{N}),\gamma^{\prime},F~]

This is proven analogously to the continuity in lemma
36
.
∎

Lemma 43

(Admissible Set)
.

Defining
S^{\alpha}_{i}
analogous to lemma
37
as

\begin{array}[]{l}S^{\alpha}_{i}:=\{[\bar{C}]\in\mathbb{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}~|~\exists C\in[\bar{C}]\ldotp\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})\ldotp\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)\ldotp\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}\}\end{array}

then the set
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, defined as

\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}:=\prod\limits_{i\in I}S^{\alpha}_{i}

is an admissible subset of
(\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\gamma^{\prime}},\sqsubseteq_{n})
.

Proof.

Least Element.
The one-dimensional least element
\bot_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, which is equivalent to the command

\mathtt{while}\ (\mathtt{true})\ \mathtt{skip}
, trivially semantically satisfies any over-approximating quadruple. We therefore have
\bot_{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

Chain-Closure.
Assume a chain
\big([\bar{C}](m)\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
. Then,
\big([\bar{C}^{i}](m)\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a chain in
S^{\alpha}_{i}
for all
i\in I
. Since
S^{\alpha}_{i}
is chain-closed (Lemma
37
), we have
\bigsqcup(\big([\bar{C}^{i}](m)\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}|m\in\mathbb{N})\in S^{\alpha}_{i}
. The desired result is then obtained by applying the definition of
\bigsqcup
.
∎

Lemma 44

(n-Scott Condition)
.

Under the assumption that for all
i\in I
:

\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})\ldotp\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t^{\prime}

and

\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t^{\prime}

it holds that

G(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha})\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}

Proof.

Assume
[\bar{C}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, i.e.
[\bar{C}^{i}]\in S^{\alpha}_{i}
, then for all
i\in I
there exists a
C^{i}\in[\bar{C}^{i}]
such that

\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})\ldotp\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right)}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)\ldotp\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C^{i}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}

We aim to show
[H(C)]\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, i.e. for all
i\in I

[h_{i}(C)]\in S^{\alpha}_{i}

where
C:=(C^{1},...,C^{n})
. What we prove is the general claim

\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array})}\ {C_{i}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array})}~\Longrightarrow~\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {C_{i}[C,\gamma^{\prime},F]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}\end{array}\right\}}

which is shown by induction over the structure of
C_{i}
analogously to the proof of Lemma
38
. Then, instantiation with the existentially quantified
t^{\prime}
yields the desired result.
∎

Lemma 45

(Auxiliary lemma)
.

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in I
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
it holds that

\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})\leq m\land\sigma,C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}~\Longleftrightarrow~\sigma,C_{i}[H^{m}(C),\gamma^{\prime},F]\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}

This lemma is proven analogously to lemma
40
. We now proceed to the last step of the proof:

Lemma 46

(n-Dimensional Last Step)
.

It holds that

\left(\begin{array}[]{l}C_{1}\\
\vdots\\
C_{n}\end{array}\right)\in\text{\it lfp}(G)

Proof.

Given the known identities about least fixpoints and the definitions of
G
and
H
, we obtain:

\text{\it lfp}(G)=\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}G^{m}(\bot_{n})=\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}[H^{m}(\mathtt{wts}^{n})]=[\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}H^{m}(\mathtt{wts}^{n})],

where
\mathtt{wts}^{n}
denotes the
n
-tuple whose every component is
\mathtt{while}\ (\mathtt{true})\ \mathtt{skip}
. For this proof, given a vector
v
, we write
(v)_{i}
to denote its
i
-th component.
Assume
i\in I
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{State}
such that
\sigma,C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime}
. This implies that the execution terminates and therefore there exists
m>0
such that
\mathtt{depth}_{{F}}(\sigma,C_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}\sigma^{\prime})\leq m
. Lemma
45
then yields

\begin{array}[]{r l}&\sigma,C_{i}[H^{m-1}(\mathtt{wts}^{n})]\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,(H^{m}(\mathtt{wts}^{n}))_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}(H^{m}(\mathtt{wts}^{n}))_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\Rightarrow&\sigma,\big(\bigsqcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}H^{m}(\mathtt{wts}^{n})\big)_{i}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

which concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem
25
and Lemmas
44
and
46
then imply for all
i\in I
that
[C_{i}]\in S^{\alpha}_{i}
.

Appendix E
Soundness: Environment Formation

To prove Theorem
18
, we first prove the following, slightly weaker, lemma:

Lemma 47
.

Assume an environment
(\gamma,\Gamma)
, a finite set of indices
I=\{1,...,n\}
and
let
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma[f_{i}\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}_{i}},C_{i},\mathsf{E}_{i})|i\in I]
,
with
f_{i}\not\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)
for all
i\in I
.
Then, for any ordinal
\alpha
, defining

\Gamma(\alpha)=\Gamma[f_{i}\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathcal{Q}_{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\mid{\beta<\alpha}\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\mid{\beta\leq\alpha}\}\mid i\in I],

and assuming that

\forall i\in I,\alpha\ldotp\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathcal{Q}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t^{\prime}

(4)

and

\forall i\in I,\alpha\ldotp\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t^{\prime}

(5)

it holds that

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)\implies(\forall\alpha.\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))).

Proof.

By transfinite induction on the ordinal
\alpha
, reasoning about the zero, successor and limit-ordinal cases.

Zero case.

When
\alpha=0
, by definition we have
\Gamma(0)=\Gamma[f_{i}\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}|i\in I]
. Let
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma^{\prime})
: then,
if
f\neq f_{i},\forall i\in I
and for any
t\in(\Gamma(0))(f)
, it holds that
t\in\Gamma(f)
and from
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
we obtain the existence of a
t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)
such that
\models C:t^{\prime}
.

Otherwise,
f=f_{i}
for some
i\in I
, in which case
(\Gamma(0))(f)
is a singleton set only holding the specification
t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
.
We instantiate the
n
-dimensional Scott induction of §
D.2
with
\alpha=0
, which yields

\forall i\in I\ldotp[C_{i}]\in S^{\alpha}_{i}

that is, there exists a
C\in[C_{i}]
such that

\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}

The assumption
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
implies
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}
. Therefore,
C
only calls on functions from
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)
, allowing us to arbitrarily extend the domain of
\gamma
, yielding
\gamma^{\prime}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\end{array})}\ {C}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}
, that is:

\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\forall\theta,s,h,h_{f},o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}.~\theta,s,h\models P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\implies\\
\qquad(s,h\uplus h_{f}),{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\implies\\
\qquad\quad o\neq\mathit{miss}\land(\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\mathsf{False})\end{array}

As
C
and
C_{i}
are behaviourally equivalent, we trivially obtain the same statement for
C_{i}
, i.e.

\gamma^{\prime}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(0)\end{array})}\ {C_{i}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}

which concludes this case of the proof.

Successor case.

In the successor case, we assume the inductive hypothesis (IH)
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))
for an arbitrary ordinal
\alpha
, and we need to prove that
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha+1))
. By definition, (H1)
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha))=\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha+1))
and (H2)
(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)\subset(\Gamma(\alpha+1))(f)
holds for all
f
. Now, let
f
be in
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha+1))
. From the definition, we obtain that

(\Gamma(\alpha+1))(f)=\begin{cases}\Gamma(f),&\textrm{ for }f\neq f_{i},\forall i\in I\\
(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\},&\textrm{ for }f=f_{i},\textrm{ for some }i\in I\\
\hskip 11.38092pt\cup~\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha+1)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}&\end{cases}

We prove the various cases separately.
First, if
f\neq f_{i}\;\forall i\in I
, then the inductive hypothesis
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))
implies that

\forall f\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}{(\Gamma(\alpha))},t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f).~f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{C;\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\}\in\gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t).~\gamma^{\prime}\models C:t^{\prime}

Since in this case
(\Gamma(\alpha+1))(f)=\Gamma(f)=(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)
and
\gamma(f)=\gamma^{\prime}(f)
since the domains of
\gamma
and
\Gamma
coincide, we immediately obtain the desired

\forall t\in(\Gamma(\alpha+1))(f)\ldotp\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)\ldotp\gamma^{\prime}\models C:t^{\prime}.

Next, let
f=f_{i}
for some
i\in I
and let
t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})
. Then, the inductive hypothesis
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))
immediately implies the existence of a
t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)
such that
\gamma^{\prime}\models C_{i}:t^{\prime}
.

Next, let
t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}
.
From (
4
), we know that there exists a
t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)
such that

\gamma^{\prime}\vdash C_{i}:t^{\prime}
. From there,
given Theorem
17
and the inductive hypothesis
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))
, we obtain that
\gamma^{\prime}\models C_{i}:t^{\prime}
.

Finally, let
t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha+1)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
.
Per definition, we have

\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha+1)\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}
, where
\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{pv}(C_{i})\backslash\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}_{i}\}
.
We instantiate the
n
-dimensional Scott induction from §
D.2
with
\alpha+1
and obtain

[C_{i}]\in s_{i}^{\alpha+1}

Analogously to the argumentation in the zero case, we obtain the desired

\gamma^{\prime}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha+1)\end{array})}\ {C_{i}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}

concluding the successor case.

Limit Ordinal Case.
Given an arbitrary limit ordinal
\alpha
, we assume the inductive hypothesis (IH)
\forall\lambda<\alpha.~\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\lambda))
, and our goal is to prove that
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha))
.

Let
f
be an arbitrary element from
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha))
. First, if
f\neq f_{i},\forall i\in I
, then we have
(\Gamma(\alpha))(f)=\Gamma(f)
and
\gamma^{\prime}(f)=\gamma(f)
. The reasoning for this case is the same as for the first part of the successor case.
Otherwise, we have that
f=f_{i}
for some
i\in I
.
For this case, we first prove that the following two sets are equal:

\begin{array}[]{l}A=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\;|\;\beta<\alpha\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\;|\;\beta\leq\alpha\}\text{~and~}\\
B=\big(\bigcup\limits_{\beta<\alpha}(\Gamma(\beta))(f_{i})\big)\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}.\end{array}

For the left-to-right inclusion, let
t\in A
.

Case 1.

t=(P^{i}(\lambda))~({\mathcal{Q}}^{i}(\lambda))
for some
\lambda<\alpha
. As
\alpha
is a limit ordinal, there exists another ordinal
\beta
such that
\lambda<\beta<\alpha
. By construction it holds that
t\in(\Gamma(\beta))(f_{i})
and hence
t\in B
.

Case 2.

t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\lambda)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
for some
\lambda<\alpha
. Per construction, we have
t\in(\Gamma(\lambda))(f_{i})
and therefore
t\in B
.

Case 3.

t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
. Trivially, we have
t\in B
.

For the right-to-left inclusion, let
t\in B
.

Case 1.

t\in(\Gamma(\lambda))(f_{i})
for some
\lambda<\alpha
. Then, per construction,
t\in A
.

Case 2.

t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
. Again, per construction,
t\in A
.

This yields the equality of
A
and
B
, which gives us that

(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})=\big(\bigcup\limits_{\beta<\alpha}(\Gamma(\beta))(f_{i})\big)\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}.

Now, let
t\in(\Gamma(\alpha))(f_{i})
, and consider the following two cases:

Case 1.

t\in(\Gamma(\lambda))(f_{i})
for some
\lambda<\alpha
. The inductive hypothesis implies
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\lambda))
and therefore that
\gamma^{\prime}\models C_{i}:t^{\prime}
for some
t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}(t)
.

Case 2.

t={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}
. This is proven by instantiating the
n
-dimensional Scott induction from §
D.2
with
\alpha
, which analogously to the zero case, yields
\gamma^{\prime}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array})}\ {C_{i}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array})}
, which conludes the proof.
∎

Before the final soundness proof, we require one further lemma:

Lemma 48

(Existentialisation)
.

Let
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
,
\gamma(f)=(\vec{\mathsf{x}},C_{f},\mathsf{E}^{\prime})
, and let

X=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(x)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\end{array}\right)}~|~x\in\mathcal{O}\}
. Then, if
X\subseteq\Gamma(f)
, it holds that
\models(\gamma,\bar{\Gamma})
, where

\begin{array}[]{r l}\bar{\Gamma}=&\Gamma[f\mapsto(\Gamma(f)\\
&\quad\quad\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\}\setminus X]\end{array}

Proof.

We need to prove that

\exists t\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)})\ldotp\gamma\models C_{f}:t

Over-approximation.

Per construction, we know that the pre-condition of any internalisation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}
equals
(\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
, where
\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{pv}(C)\backslash\mathtt{pv}(\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})
, which is equivalent to
\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
. We assume
\theta,s,h,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime},h_{f},o
such that

(O1)

\theta,s,h\models\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}

(O2)

(s,h\uplus h_{f}),C_{f}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

and aim to show:

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\bar{Q}_{\epsilon}

where
\bar{Q}_{\epsilon}
is an internal postcondition of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}
.

•

(O1) implies that
\exists v\in\mathcal{O}\ldotp\theta[x\rightarrow v],s,h\models P(x)\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
.

•

The assumptions of the lemma then imply that
(O3)

o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}\ldotp h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta[x\rightarrow v],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{\prime}_{o}
for some
Q^{\prime}_{o}
that is an internal post-condition of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(x)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\end{array}\right)}
, where
x\in\mathcal{O}
, i.e. we have either

\begin{array}[]{l c}\textbf{(O4a)}&\qquad(o=ok)\land(Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)~\Leftrightarrow~\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}])\\
\textbf{(O4b)}&\qquad(o=err)\land(Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)~\Leftrightarrow~\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}])\end{array}

where, without loss of generality, we may assume that
x\notin\vec{p}
.

•

(O3) implies that
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists x\ldotp Q^{\prime}_{o}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}
.

•

To conclude the OX direction of the proof, we show that
\exists x\ldotp Q^{\prime}_{o}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}
is an internal post-condition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}

which is implied by (O4a) in case of successful, and by (O4b) in case of erroneous termination:

\displaystyle\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists x\ldotp\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]

\displaystyle~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{p}\ldotp(\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}])

\displaystyle~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{p}\ldotp(\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]

\displaystyle\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists x\ldotp\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{p}\ldotp\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle~\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{p}\ldotp(\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]

This concludes the OX direction.

Under-approximation.
We assume
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},h_{f},o
such that
h^{\prime}\sharp h_{f}
and
\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models\exists x\ldotp Q^{\prime}_{o}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}
, where
Q^{\prime}_{o}
is obtained from the OX case. This implies the existence of a
v\in\mathcal{O}
such that

\theta[x\rightarrow v],s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\models Q^{\prime}_{o}

From the assumptions, we obtain the existence of
s,h
such that
\theta[x\rightarrow v],s,h\models P(x)\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}
, which implies

\theta,s,h\models\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

This concludes the proof as the last assertion is the (only) internal pre-condition of of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp P(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists x\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}(x)\mathrel{\star}{x\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\end{array}\right)}
.

∎

With these lemmas, we can now easily prove
\cref
thm:envsound:

See
18

Proof of Theorem
18
.

By induction on
\vdash(\gamma,\Gamma)
. When the last rule applied was the base rule for environments, we have that
(\gamma,\Gamma)=(\emptyset,\emptyset)
, meaning that
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma)
is empty, and the statement to prove is trivially true.
Otherwise,
we assume the following hypotheses and inductive hypothesis:

(H1)

\vdash(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma[f_{i}\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}_{i}},C_{i},\mathsf{E}_{i})|i\in I]
,
with
f_{i}\not\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)
for all
i\in I

(H3)

\Gamma(\alpha)=\Gamma[f_{i}\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\mid{\beta<\alpha}\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\beta)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\mid\beta\leq\alpha\}]_{i\in I}

(H4)

\forall i\in I,\alpha\in\mathcal{O}\ldotp\exists t\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\big).~\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t

(H5)

\forall i\in I,\alpha\in\mathcal{O}\ldotp\exists t\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f_{i}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}).~\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash C_{i}:t

(IH)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

We will first prove that
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))
, that is,

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma^{\prime})\land\forall f,\vec{\mathsf{x}},C,\mathsf{E}\ldotp f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{C;\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\}\in\gamma^{\prime}\\
\qquad\Rightarrow(\forall t\ldotp t\in(\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))(f)\\
\qquad\qquad\Rightarrow\exists t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)\ldotp\gamma^{\prime}\models C:t^{\prime})\end{array}

where the notation
\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha)
denotes the function which maps
f
to the set
\cup_{\alpha}((\Gamma(\alpha))(f))
. The fist conjunct holds since Lemma
47
implies that
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha))\subseteq\gamma^{\prime}
for all
\alpha
. Now, assume
f,\vec{\mathsf{x}},C,\mathsf{E},t
such that

(H6)

f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{C;\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\}\in\gamma^{\prime}

(H7)

t\in(\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))(f)

By (H7), we have that there exists
\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}
such that
(H8)

t\in(\Gamma(\alpha^{\prime}))(f)
.
Then, from Lemma
47
applied to (IH), (H2), (H4), and (H5), we obtain

(H9)

\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma(\alpha^{\prime}))
. By construction, we know that
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))=\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha^{\prime}))
, meaning that
(H10)

f\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma(\alpha^{\prime}))
. Therefore, instantiating (H9) with (H10), (H8), and (H6), we obtain that there exists
t^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(t)
such that
\models C:t^{\prime}
, which implies
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha))
. Defining

•

P^{i}=\exists\alpha\ldotp P^{i}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}{\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

•

P_{\infty}^{i}=\exists\alpha\ldotp P_{\infty}^{i}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}{\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

•

Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{i}=\exists\alpha\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{i}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}{\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

•

Q_{\mathit{err}}^{i}=\exists\alpha\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{i}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}{\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime\prime}:=\Gamma[f_{i}\mapsto\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P^{i}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{i}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{i}\end{array}\right)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{\infty}^{i}\end{array}\right)}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{False}\end{array}\right)}\}]_{i\in I}
,

and applying Lemma
48
twice to
\cup_{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha)
(once to the set of partially terminating specifications and once to the set of non-terminating specifications), we obtain
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, concluding the soundness proof.
∎

Appendix F
Further Examples

F.1
Mutual Recursion: Even/Odd

ESL allows us to reason about mutually recursive function. We illustrate this by using a simple example consisting of two functions which determine whether a natural number is even or odd, whose implementations are given below.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{isEven}(\mathsf{n})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{n}=0)\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{true}\\
\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{n}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{n}-1;\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{isOdd}(\mathsf{n})\\
\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{b}\\
\}\end{array}\quad\quad\quad\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{isOdd}(\mathsf{n})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{n}=0)\ \{;\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{false}\\
\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{n}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{n}-1;\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{isEven}(\mathsf{n})\\
\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{b}\\
\}\end{array}

To reason about these two functions, we introduce two (also mutually recursive) predicates:

\begin{array}[]{l@{~\defeq~}l}\mathsf{even}(n)\hfil~\triangleq&n=0\vee\mathsf{odd}(n-1)\\
\mathsf{odd}(n)\hfil~\triangleq&n=1\vee\mathsf{even}(n-1).\\
\end{array}

and give the pre-condition and the external (
Q_{even}(\alpha)
and
Q_{odd}(\alpha)
) and internal (
Q^{\prime}_{even}(\alpha)
and
Q^{\prime}_{odd}(\alpha)
) post-conditions for the two functions, noting that both share the same pre-condition
P(\alpha)
, and that we again use
\alpha=n
for the decreasing measure, just as in the list length case:

\begin{array}[]{r@{~}c@{~}l}P(\alpha)&\triangleq&\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\\[5.69054pt]
Q_{even}(\alpha)&\triangleq&(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
Q_{odd}(\alpha)&\triangleq&(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\[5.69054pt]
Q^{\prime}_{even}(\alpha)&\triangleq&(\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
&&\quad\lor(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
&&\quad\quad\lor(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
Q^{\prime}_{odd}(\alpha)&\triangleq&(\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
&&\quad\lor(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
&&\quad\quad\lor(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
\end{array}

We further assume a well-formed environment
(\gamma,\Gamma)
such that
\mathtt{isEven},\mathtt{isOdd}\not\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)
and extend it as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r c l}\gamma^{\prime}&\triangleq&\gamma[\mathtt{isEven}\mapsto(\{\mathsf{n}\},C_{even},\mathsf{b}),\mathtt{isOdd}\mapsto(\{\mathsf{n}\},C_{odd},\mathsf{b})]\\
\Gamma(\alpha)&\triangleq&\Gamma[\mathtt{isEven}\mapsto\{(P(\beta))~(\mathit{ok}:Q_{even}(\beta))\mid{\beta<\alpha}\},\\
&&\quad\mathtt{isOdd}\mapsto\{(P(\beta))~(\mathit{ok}:Q_{odd}(\beta))\mid{\beta<\alpha}\}]\end{array}

where
C_{even}
and
C_{odd}
denote the appropriate function bodies. Our goal is to prove

\begin{array}[]{c}\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(\alpha)\end{array})}\ {C_{even}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q^{\prime}_{even}(\alpha)\end{array})}\\
\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P(\alpha)\end{array})}\ {C_{odd}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q^{\prime}_{odd}(\alpha)\end{array})}\end{array}

which we do in the proof sketches given below, first for
\mathtt{isEven}
and then for
\mathtt{isOdd}
:

\small\begin{array}[]{r@{~}l}\Gamma(\alpha)~\vdash&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}P(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}{n\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{n}=0)\ \{\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{true}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\end{array}~)}\\
&\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n\mathbin{>}0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{n}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{n}-1\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}n\mathbin{>}0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}{n-1\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}}\mathrel{\star}n-1=\alpha-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}// as $\alpha-1<\alpha$, we can apply the specification for $\alpha-1$}}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{isOdd}(\mathsf{n})\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\exists b\ldotp\big((b=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n-1))\vee(b=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n-1))\big)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=b\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\big((\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\big)\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\end{array}~)}\\
&\}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}(\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)~\vee\\
(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\end{array}~\right)}\end{array}

\small\begin{array}[]{r@{~}l}\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}P(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}{n\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{n}=0)\ \{\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{false}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\end{array}~)}\\
&\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n\mathbin{>}0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{n}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{n}-1\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}n\mathbin{>}0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}{n-1\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}}\mathrel{\star}n-1=\alpha-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}// since $\alpha-1<\alpha$, we can apply the specification for $\alpha-1$}}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{b}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{isEven}(\mathsf{n})\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\exists b\ldotp\big((b=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n-1))\vee(b=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n-1))\big)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=b\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\big((\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\big)\end{array}~)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\end{array}~)}\\
&\}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}(\mathsf{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false})\vee(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)~\vee\\
(\mathsf{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\end{array}~\right)}\end{array}

To complete the proof, we need to show that
Q_{i}\Leftrightarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q^{\prime}_{i}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{b}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]
for
i\in\{\text{even, odd}\}
. As the two cases are analogous, we only show the
\mathtt{even}
case in detail:

\begin{array}[]{l l}&\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q^{\prime}_{even}(\alpha)[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\\
\Leftrightarrow&\exists p_{n},p_{b}.\begin{array}[]{l}((p_{n}=n\mathrel{\star}n=0\mathrel{\star}p_{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)~\vee\\
(p_{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}p_{b}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)~\vee\\
(p_{n}=n-1\mathrel{\star}p_{b}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha))\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=p_{b}\\
\end{array}\\
\Leftrightarrow&(n=0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(n~\mathbin{>}~0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)~\vee\\
&(n~\mathbin{>}~0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
\Leftrightarrow&(n~\mathbin{>}~0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(n\in\mathbb{N}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
\Leftrightarrow&(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{false}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\vee(\mathsf{ret}=\mathtt{true}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(n)\mathrel{\star}n=\alpha)\\
\Leftrightarrow&Q_{even}(\alpha)\end{array}

F.2
More Complex Mutual Recursion: Even/Odd/List Length

In the examples in the main body and the mutually recursive example in the previous section, the choice of the measure is straightforward. For, e.g., list length and list free, we traverse a non-cyclic list, therefore decreasing the distance to the end of the list in every step. For even/odd, each function decreases the function argument before passing it on to the other function, therefore also creating a natural measure.

In the real world, however, we might come across clusters of mutually recursive functions where not every function reduces the obvious measure (e.g., wrapper functions). As long as any function call terminates, we can still reason about such clusters by defining an appropriate measure. To illustrate this, we will look at a collection of three functions, which compute the length of a list in a convoluted, mutually-recursive way:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{LL}(\mathsf{x})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{x}=\mathtt{null})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }0;\\
\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{v}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}];\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{even}(v))\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{r}\\
\}\\
\end{array}\hskip 42.67912pt\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{f}(\mathsf{x})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\mathsf{v}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}];\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{even}(v))\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}+1];\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{LL}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{r}+1;\\
\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{r}\\
\}\\
\\
\\
\end{array}\hskip 42.67912pt\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{g}(\mathsf{x})\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\mathsf{v}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}];\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{odd}(v))\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}+1];\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathtt{LL}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{r}+1;\\
\quad\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{\\
\quad\!\!\quad\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{x});\\
\quad\!\!\};\\
\quad\!\!\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{r}\\
\}\\
\\
\\
\end{array}

Intuitively, whenever either of the functions is called with an argument
\mathsf{x}
, which is the head of a (non-cyclic) list, it computes the length of the list. The
\mathtt{LL}
function calls
g
if the first value of the list is even and
f
otherwise. The function
g
, however, does the same test and calls
f
if the input was even. Otherwise, it moves one element down the list and calls
\mathtt{LL}
on the now shortened list. The function
f
moves down the list by one element and calls
\mathtt{LL}
on the shortened list if the first value is even, and calls
g
on the initial list, if not.

As the functions branch on whether or not values of the list are divisible by 2, we adjust the
\mathsf{list}(x,\mathit{vs})
predicate slightly to include the condition that
\mathit{vs}
is a list of natural numbers:

\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\triangleq(x=\mathtt{null}\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=\epsilon)\vee(\exists v,x,\mathit{vs}^{\prime}\ldotp x\mapsto v,x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}v\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x^{\prime},\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})

and furthermore require a trivial property of the previously introduced
\mathsf{even}(n)
and
\mathsf{odd}(n)
predicates, stating that

\mathsf{even}(v)\vee\mathsf{odd}(v)\Leftrightarrow v\in\mathbb{N}
. Assuming a valid environment
\vdash(\gamma,\Gamma)
, we extend it as follows

\begin{array}[]{r c l}\gamma^{\prime}&\triangleq&\gamma[\mathtt{LL}\mapsto(\{\mathsf{x}\},C_{LL},\mathsf{r}),\quad\mathtt{f}\mapsto(\{\mathsf{x}\},C_{f},\mathsf{r}),\quad\mathtt{g}\mapsto(\{\mathsf{x}\},C_{g},\mathsf{r})]\\
\Gamma(\alpha)&\triangleq&\Gamma[\mathtt{LL}\mapsto\{(P_{LL}(\beta))(ok:Q_{LL})|\beta<\alpha\},\\
&&\quad\quad\mathtt{f}\mapsto\{(P_{f}(\beta))(ok:Q_{f})|\beta<\alpha\},\mathtt{g}\mapsto\{(P_{g}(\beta))(ok:Q_{g})|\beta<\alpha\}]\end{array}

Furthermore, we define

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\Gamma[\mathtt{LL}\mapsto\{(\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}{(3|\mathit{vs}|+2)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\\
\hskip 85.35826pt(ok:\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=vs\mathrel{\star}{(3|\mathit{vs}|+2)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\},\\
\hskip 38.41139pt\mathtt{f}\mapsto\{(\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}{3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+(v\bmod 2)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\\
\hskip 85.35826pt(ok:\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\mathrel{\star}{3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+(v\bmod 2))\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}}\},\\
\hskip 38.41139pt\mathtt{g}\mapsto\{(\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}{3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+1-(v\bmod 2)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\\
\hskip 85.35826pt(ok:\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\mathrel{\star}{3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+1-(v\bmod 2)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{O}})\}]\end{array}

and wish to prove
\vdash(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
. To this end, we prove the following three specifications:

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{LL}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array})}\ {C_{LL}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q^{\prime}_{LL}(\alpha)\end{array})}\\
\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{f}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array})}\ {C_{f}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q^{\prime}_{f}(\alpha)\end{array})}\\
\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P_{g}(\alpha)\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array})}\ {C_{g}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q^{\prime}_{g}(\alpha)\end{array})}\end{array}

where
C_{LL}
,
C_{f}
, and
C_{g}
denote the appropriate function bodies, and the function pre-conditions, capturing the function pre-conditions (with and without measure) and post-conditions (internal and external), are defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{~}c@{~}l}P_{LL}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\\
P_{f}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+(v\bmod 2)\\
P_{g}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+1-(v\bmod 2)\\[5.69054pt]
Q^{\prime}_{LL}(\alpha)&=&(x=\mathtt{null}\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=\epsilon\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=|\mathit{vs}|\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2)~\vee\\
&&(\exists v,\mathit{vs}^{\prime}\ldotp\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{v}=v\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=|\mathit{vs}|\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2)\\
Q^{\prime}_{f}(\alpha)&=&\exists x^{\prime}\ldotp x\mapsto v,x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x^{\prime},\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{v}=v\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\\[-1.42262pt]
&&\mathrel{\star}(\mathsf{x}=x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(v)\vee\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(v))\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+(v\bmod 2)\\
Q^{\prime}_{g}(\alpha)&=&\exists x^{\prime}\ldotp x\mapsto v,x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x^{\prime},\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{v}=v\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\\
&&\mathrel{\star}(\mathsf{x}=x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{odd}(v)\vee\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{even}(v))\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+1-(v\bmod 2)\\[5.69054pt]
Q_{LL}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=|\mathit{vs}|\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\\
Q_{f}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+(v\bmod 2)\\
Q_{g}(\alpha)&=&\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}|+1-(v\bmod 2)\\
\end{array}

We give the proof sketch for list length and
\mathtt{f}
below. The proof sketch for
\mathtt{g}
is analogous to that of
\mathtt{f}
.

\displaystyle\Gamma(\alpha)\vdash

\displaystyle~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathtt{if}\ (\mathsf{x}=\mathtt{null})\ \{

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v},\mathsf{x}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{r}\mathtt{\ :=\ }0;

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x,\mathit{vs})\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=0\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}x=\mathtt{null}\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=\epsilon\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r}=|\mathit{vs}|\mathrel{\star}\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\}\ \mathtt{else}\ \{

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(~\begin{array}[]{@{} l @{}}\exists v,x^{\prime},\mathit{vs}^{\prime}\ldotp x\mapsto v,x^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}v\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{N}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{list_{\mathbb{N}}}(x^{\prime},\mathit{vs}^{\prime})\mathrel{\star}\mathit{vs}=v:\mathit{vs}^{\prime}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{x}=x\mathrel{\star}\\
\alpha=3|\mathit{vs}|+2\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{r},\mathsf{v}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}~\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\hskip 9.24994pt\!\!\mathsf{v}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{x}];
